The Best Window Insulation Kits of 2026, Tested and Reviewed

📅 2026-11-08  |  Read Source →

100+ Most Unique and Practical Gifts 🎁 for Homeowners and DIYers

Published Nov 8, 2026 9:00 AM EST

We may earn revenue from the products available on this page and participate in affiliate programs. Learn More ›

Window insulation kits offer an affordable way to cut the cost of your energy bill and make your home less drafty. For those who can’t afford to replace their windows, these kits are a great alternative.

Most window insulation kits work the same way, by attaching a sheet of heat-shrink plastic or other insulating material to the window panes with double-sided tape or magnetic strips. But since they don’t all perform equally well, we put several of the most popular options to the test in our own homes. Based on those tests, we chose the 3M Indoor Window Insulator Kit as the best overall option for its relatively easy installation and the fact that a single kit covers multiple windows.

Read on for shopping considerations when selecting this type of window treatment and to learn why these are among the best window insulation kits available.

Bottom line: Window insulation kits are an affordable way to reduce energy costs and drafts, with the 3M Indoor Kit leading the pack for its ease and coverage.

Windows that have been treated with insulation cannot usually be opened, meaning these kits are a seasonal solution to drafty windows. The following recommendations are among the best options available in their respective categories.

This 3M insulator kit comes with enough film to cover up to five windows measuring 5 feet long by 3 feet wide. This relatively thick film provides enhanced insulation thanks to its thickness of 0.75 mils (a unit of measurement equal to a thousandth of an inch). The film comes in one cut-to-fit sheet that measures 17.5 feet long by 5.16 feet wide. This kit also comes with a 27.7-yard roll of double-sided tape to facilitate installation and ensure it stays in place all season.

This was my favorite kit to install. I thought I would prefer the rolls of plastic sheeting, and it is true that this one was tricky to lay out and cut to size, but nonetheless, this kit allowed me to reposition the plastic a few times before pressing it down and heating it. Also, the kit does cover a lot of square footage, but the ends of the sheet can be tough, so it’s important to take your time. After I finished installing the kit, removing wrinkles was extremely easy. This kit provided the best results alongside the other 3M products that use the same plastic.

Read our full review: 3M Indoor Window Insulator Kit

What our tester says: “This was my favorite kit because I could reposition the film before pressing it into place, even though laying out and cutting the large sheet took some patience. Once it was up, the wrinkles came out easily with heat, and the finished results were among the best.”—Tom Scalisi, Product Reviews tester and writer

Get the 3M indoor window insulation kit at Amazon.

Cozy as the winter months can be, the cold outside can be brutal.

To keep a home insulated and warm in the winter, consider the Duck Max Strength window kit. It comes with everything required for insulating 5-foot-long by 3-foot-wide windows in bedrooms, offices, kitchens, and more, including film, tape, and an instruction manual. But it can be cut to fit smaller windows if necessary.

Made with puncture-resistant shrink film, this 126-inch-long by 62-inch-wide indoor option is perfect for high-traffic homes with children and pets. It’s easy to install and remove cleanly, making it suitable for first timers. For added reliability, this kit can be used on painted wood, aluminum, vinyl, and finished/varnished wood, but it can’t be used on plaster, drywall, or unfinished wood.

The Duck Brand window kit’s film is considerably thicker than other competing options, so I installed it over the window my 80-pound lab-pointer mix likes to jump at. To be fair, no film stood a chance against my dog, but it did stick to the windowsill that he has effectively destroyed over the past year. I was able to easily repair any damage with packing tape. Also, the Duck kits come in rolls, which are easy to cut to width and then drop down over the window for easy unfolding. It was really difficult to get the wrinkles out (it took over 30 minutes of heating with a blow dryer to get it reasonably clear), but it was easy to remove once I could get under the tape and pull it back.

Get the Duck Brand Max Strength window insulation kit at Amazon.

Those who want to insulate on a budget can trust this shrink film kit from Duck Brand. With its crystal-clear shrink film and included double-sided insulating tape, this option can reduce energy consumption. At 210 inches long by 62 inches wide, this large film can be cut if required but works best for windows that measure 5 feet long by 3 feet wide. For convenience, this option is also offered in various size options.

This pick can also be applied to painted wood, aluminum, vinyl, and finished wood for more insulation, but it isn’t suitable for plaster, drywall, or unfinished wood varieties.

The kit was fairly easy to install, and it seemed reasonably durable. The roll-out installation made it manageable to unravel, and it featured quality adhesive tape that stuck well and was easy to remove the backing from. While it’s similar in thickness to most of the films we tested, it felt smoother out of the box. It also had a more substantial, higher-quality feel. Unfortunately, it was difficult to remove the existing wrinkles, and there were marks from the manufacturing plant in the film that I could not heat out.

Get the Duck Brand shrink film window insulation kit at Amazon.

If shoppers prefer to install insulating film on the outside of windows, they’ll want a long-lasting product that can hold up to the elements. This outdoor-use window film was created by 3M for that very purpose. Capable of covering up to two 5-foot-long by 3-foot-wide windows, 3M’s kit comes with a sheet of 0.75 mil-thick film that’s 7 feet long and 5.16 feet wide. It also includes just under 14 yards of high-quality double-sided tape. This kit installs like an indoor window film kit: cut, tape, shrink, and trim.

The only difference I could discern between this kit and the other 3M insulation kits was that the adhesive felt like it was of a better quality (though there was nothing wrong with the quality of the other kits). Otherwise, it was the same thickness with the same ease of wrinkle removal. The only challenge I found was that it was difficult and frustrating to install a sheet outside if there was any wind at all, so 3M might consider using a roll-type installation for this kit. But in my opinion, there is no reason it can’t be installed on an interior window as well, so it’s a good kit all around.

Get the 3M outdoor window insulation kit at Amazon.

New insulating tape is essential for those who love to reuse their insulation sheets. When new insulation is needed, Yotache’s high-density foam tape is a good option. This thick tape comes in two rolls, providing a combined 13 feet of length. It can be cut with ease using a scissors or blade.

The closed cell foam is made from chloroprene rubber foam and neoprene, making it weather- and dustproof, oil- and corrosion-resistant, and flame-retardant. As for the adhesive quality, this tape has a waterproof grid backing that can endure temperatures ranging from -58 to 302 degrees Fahrenheit. Though these are meant for windows, they can also be attached to furniture, cabinets, cars, boats, sports equipment, HVAC systems, plumbing, and more. Plus, there are a lot of thicknesses and lengths to choose from based on the user’s needs.

I installed the Yotache weather stripping at the bottom of one of my windows to see how well it worked. My windows are new and not leaky, so the tolerances were a little tight (though I was able to lock the sashes). However, if I did have leaky windows, I wouldn’t hesitate to buy this foam. It’s incredibly tough thanks to the fiberglass adhesive mesh on the back, yet it’s easy to cut and install. It also removes cleanly but won’t fall off on its own. Plus, there’s a benefit to being able to open the window if necessary. I found the foam too dense for my application, but I can see it making a world of difference for older windows with too much clearance.

Get the Yotache window insulation tape at Amazon.

Comfort aside, one of the most important reasons to insulate drafty windows is to save energy and lower monthly heating costs. Saving energy also benefits the environment. With Frost King’s window film, the company claims users can reduce their carbon footprint related to heating and cooling by up to 35 percent. This kit comes with enough individual sheets of plastic to cover up to nine windows measuring 5 feet long by 3 feet wide. It also includes 165 feet of double-sided tape.

This kit is very similar to other kits on the list, but that’s okay because they’re all good quality. I found that this one installed just like the others, yet with slightly smaller sheets that were a little easier to manage. The film is the same thickness and quality as most of the other kits, and considering it can cover up to nine windows, it’s a great deal. The wrinkles were very easy to remove with a blow dryer, but I did find the tape wasn’t as high quality as the kits from other brands.

Get the Frost King window insulation kit at Amazon.

The StangH Portable Thermal Insulated Blackout Curtains didn’t meet my expectations. First, the way the suction cups are affixed to the curtain with keyrings is extremely frustrating and time-consuming. Also, unless the glass is warm, sticking a suction cup to a cold surface is very difficult. This curtain also looks terrible from the outside, as the suction cups are extremely visible. A standard thermal curtain will likely work almost as well, look much nicer, and be easier to install.

I wanted to ensure that any window insulation kit we recommended was truly effective and easy to install, so I put each of these kits to the test. I installed each kit according to the manufacturer’s instructions to see how easy they were to install and to determine the components’ quality. I also tested how well the plastic shrank and how easy the wrinkles were to remove. I left them on the windows for 2 days to see how well they worked (I even left a few on permanently to get through the winter). I then removed them to determine what that process was like and how much cleanup there would be.

It’s worth noting that without a laboratory full of expensive machinery and repeatable, consistent conditions, it’s hard to objectively determine which window insulation kits insulated better. However, all the kits we included on this list were effective at stopping any noticeable air movement or condensation.

There are a number of factors to consider when shopping for window insulation kits. They’re available in different foil types, such as plastic, magnetic, and reflective. Features like film thickness, dimensions, reusability, and installation requirements will determine which insulation kit is most suitable for your home.

Several window insulation product types are on the market: plastic film, magnetic barriers, and reflective foil insulation.

Windows come in all sizes, so look for kits with enough film to cover your windows. Most kits come with enough plastic sheets and double-sided tape to seal several windows. It’s essential for shoppers to know their windows’ dimensions to determine which kit will work for their home.

Window insulation kits create a trap for air between the window and the film. That trap is called an air lock. Double-sided tape, which holds the film in place on the window pane, will create an air lock. By pressing the film firmly onto the tape, users can create a strong bond that keeps drafts out.

To create a long-lasting seal, durable film is a must. Durability is often determined by the film’s thickness. Thick film offers more resistance to punctures from kids or pets. It’s also easier to apply correctly, thus establishing a good seal. Thick film does a better job of insulating windows. Standard window film is about 19 micrometers (μm) thick, while heavy-duty film can be as thick as 38 μm.

Some homes have only one or two truly drafty windows, while others have a slew of them. Choose a kit that has enough film to cover all the drafty windows. Many kits come in multipacks that can cover up to 10 windows. They come with sheets of film that can be cut to size, so every window that needs insulation can be covered.

Window insulation kits are meant to be temporary solutions to drafty windows. The good news is that most of these kits are reusable, so buyers won’t have to purchase a new kit every year. Films 19 μm or thicker can be peeled off the double-sided tape, folded up, and stored when winter is over. The double-sided tape won’t be reusable, but a roll of double-sided tape is cheaper than a houseful of window kits.

The best window insulation kits are effective, affordable, and easy to install. Most kits are installed by placing double-sided tape around the perimeter of the window jamb. Then, window film is applied to the tape. At the bottom of the window, the film is attached to a piece of tape on the sill, making a seal. Finally, the film is heated with a handheld blow dryer to create a transparent, airtight seal free from wrinkles.

Still have questions about choosing the best window insulation kits? Here’s some more information to help you decide.

Window insulation kits create an effective barrier preventing cold air from entering a home. Heat-shrink film applied to the window pane with double-sided tape creates an air gap that works like a thermal window.

Insulated curtains do a good job of keeping the cold temperatures at bay, but they don’t create airtight seals to hold back drafts like window insulation kits do. Plus, insulated curtains must be closed to work, so they block natural light. Window insulation film is usually clear, so it doesn’t block those precious rays of winter sun from shining into a home.

Some window insulation kits claim to reduce a home’s energy consumption by up to 35 percent. Users will need to install these products carefully and on every leaky window to maximize their effectiveness.

The Sky Today on Sunday, November 8: Io and Callisto race across Jupiter

📅 2026-11-08  |  Read Source →

Looking for a sky event this week? Check out our full Sky This Week column.

Rising with the stars of western Leo shortly before midnight, bright Jupiter and its Galilean moons put on a show early this morning, better visible the farther east you’re located.

Jupiter is just under 5° northwest (to the upper right) of Regulus as they rise in the east. Fainter Mars stands roughly 3° above Jupiter. You’ll want your telescope trained on the gas giant soon after rising to catch the show, which begins at 1:29 A.M. EST as a shadow appears on the planet’s eastern limb. This is Io’s shadow — but don’t be fooled by the positions of the moons you see in your scope. The moon closest to Jupiter on the east is Callisto, while the moon just east of that is Io, whose shadow we see. Ganymede lies farthest east, while Europa is currently hidden behind the planet in an occultation. The latter appears from behind the eastern limb about 1:54 A.M. EST, not long before Callisto reaches the planet and begins a transit at 2:07 A.M. EST.

Keep an eye on Io and Europa: Io passes north of Europa at 2:23 A.M. EST, then heads into its own transit about 2:42 A.M. EST. Now the race is on, as Io approaches and then catches up with Callisto as the two moons transit, passing Callisto and moving west of it within half an hour. The two moons and Io’s shadow continue to transit until 3:45 A.M. EST, when Io’s shadow leaves the western limb, and Io takes about an hour and a quarter longer before egress. Callisto continues trundling along, finally finishing its transit about 5:51 A.M. CST, with the sky now starting to brighten with twilight in the Midwest.

Sunrise: 6:37 A.M.Sunset: 4:50 P.M.Moonrise: 6:07 A.M.Moonset: 4:08 P.M.Moon Phase: New*Times for sunrise, sunset, moonrise, and moonset are given in local time from 40° N 90° W. The Moon’s illumination is given at 10 P.M. local time from the same location.

Alison Klesman is senior editor of Astronomy magazine. She holds a Ph.D. in astronomy and has studied a variety of topics, from minor planets to supermassive black holes.

XRP price fell nearly 10% in 24 hours to around $1.18, hitting an 11-week low as a sharp sell-off in global markets dragged cryptocurrencies lower. Market researcher Edu warned that XRP could fall toward $0.80 if the S&P 500 breaks below its key support level of 7,267.

The broader market downturn also pushed Bitcoin down 6% to around $75,000. Meanwhile, the Nasdaq dropped 5.5%, entering correction territory, while the S&P 500 lost more than 4%.

According to Edu, XRP’s decline is linked to broader market weakness rather than negative developments involving Ripple.

Investors are reducing exposure to risky assets as market uncertainty increases. The CBOE Volatility Index (VIX), which measures expected stock market volatility, jumped from 16 to 28 in a single session.

XRP remains closely correlated with Bitcoin and broader financial markets. As selling pressure spreads across stocks and cryptocurrencies, XRP could face further losses.

He also compared the current market conditions with 2007, when the S&P 500 reached a record high before the 2008 financial crisis. However, he clarified that this comparison does not necessarily indicate another financial crisis.

Edu identified $1.19 – $1.20 as an important support zone for XRP. The token has tested this range several times, raising concerns that buyers may struggle to defend it.

The analyst highlighted the following levels:

If XRP loses the 1.19–1.20 support zone, it could decline toward $1.10. A further breakdown below $1 could expose the token to $0.80 or even $0.77.

On the upside, a recovery above $1.27 and $1.35 could signal improving buying interest. However, Edu said he would only turn bullish on XRP again if its price reaches $1.60.

Edu also pointed to 7,267 on the S&P 500 as a critical level. This marks the index’s June low, and the index was trading less than 100 points above it when the analyst shared his assessment.

He warned that a break below 7,267 could increase selling pressure across financial markets, putting XRP’s $1.10 and $1 support levels at risk. If the S&P 500 holds this level and market sentiment improves, XRP could find support around $1.20 and attempt a recovery toward $1.27.

XRP’s decline comes despite positive developments involving Ripple.

The company reportedly received final approval from the US Office of the Comptroller of the Currency (OCC) for its national trust bank application. Ripple also announced a partnership with Robinhood involving its RLUSD stablecoin.

However, these developments have not been enough to support XRP’s price amid the broader market sell-off.

For now, XRP’s recovery may depend more on overall market conditions than Ripple’s business developments. The S&P 500’s ability to hold 7,267 could be an important factor in determining whether XRP stabilizes or faces another decline.

raw concrete and steel anchor stripped-back 1960’s apartment in zagreb

📅 2026-11-08  |  Read Source →

simple architecture approaches the renovation through subtraction rather than addition, stripping the apartment back to its raw state.

Simple edited by christina vergopoulou November 8, 2026 4 min

Sited in the western district of New Zagreb near the Sava River, Raw Apartment by Simple Architecture is an interior renovation executed within a 1960s experimental residential block. The original building was constructed using the JU61 prefabricated system, a modular assembly of load-bearing reinforced concrete walls that double as internal partitions, wrapped in an exterior envelope of corrugated steel sheet panels. The renovation adopts a subtractive methodology, stripping away accumulated surface finishes from the walls, floors, and ceilings to lay bare the prefabricated concrete substrate.

The floor plan, reconfigured by Simple Architecture Studio,  comprises a combined living and dining area, kitchen, bathroom, and single bedroom. To expand the primary living zone, two compartmentalized rooms were merged into a single open-plan volume. Because all interior walls in the JU61 system are structurally load-bearing, the removal of the dividing concrete wall required the insertion of structural steel columns and transfer beams, which are left exposed as visual records of the structural intervention.

The interior palette reflects the building’s Brutalist lineage and industrial cladding. Built-in and freestanding furniture pieces are fabricated primarily from steel and aluminum, while full-height mirrors and glass panels replace conventional partition doors to direct light through the floor plate. In the bathroom, replacing a solid concrete partition with translucent glass channels daylight from the bedroom into the previously dark core, concurrently backlighting an open wardrobe system. Floor-to-ceiling textile drapery acts as an operable partition system, offering solar modulation and adjustable spatial division against the exposed concrete shell.

name: Raw Apartment architect: Simple Architecture | @smplarchitecture

Arsenal without six at Anfield as Slegers takes full responsibility, Liverpool still missing Holland

📅 2026-11-08  |  Read Source →

To help deliver timely coverage of women's football, we may use AI tools to support content creation and editing. While we strive for accuracy and editorial quality, readers should independently verify important information.

Arsenal head to Anfield for Sunday’s WSL lunchtime game against Liverpool (12:00 UK time, live on Sky Sports) with six first team players unavailable, while Gareth Taylor is still waiting on Ceri Holland as the Reds deal with a lengthy injury list of their own.

Renée Slegers has named the same squad that drew 1-1 with Aston Villa at home last weekend, a result that halted a brief recovery after wins over Tottenham and Benfica. The Arsenal head coach has had a full week of training to work with before a demanding run that includes a Champions League trip to Barcelona and a home league meeting with London City Lionesses.

According to the Arseblog News team news round-up, goalkeeper Daphne van Domselaar is still out with a knee problem, although the Dutch international did get 45 minutes under her belt for the Under-21s this week. Mariona Caldentey and Chloe Kelly are both sidelined with hamstring injuries, Michelle Agyemang and Selina Cerci are dealing with knee issues, and Katie Reid is missing with an ankle injury.

The available group still has plenty of experience. Kim Little, back among the substitutes for the past two matches after a spell out with illness and injury, is in the squad along with Georgia Stanway and Kyra Cooney-Cross in midfield. Up front, Slegers can choose from Alessia Russo, Stina Blackstenius, Caitlin Foord and Olivia Smith, while Leah Williamson, Lotte Wubben-Moy and Steph Catley are among the defensive options.

Arsenal have conceded first in five of their eight WSL matches this season, and Slegers was open about the issue after the Villa draw, saying “it feels like we are playing the same game over and over.”

Speaking before the trip to Merseyside, she accepted the job of fixing it sits with her. “There’s motivation and ambition to do better and turn this around among the staff and players, but I take full responsibility for the environment that we need to create,” she said.

She added that training had gone well over the last week. “I believe what we need is there. We just have to make sure it comes out and expresses itself on the pitch, which is my job.”

On the home side, Taylor told his pre-match press conference that there were no new concerns, as covered on the official Liverpool FC website. Wales midfielder Ceri Holland is getting closer but is not ready for this one. “Hopefully we can get her back onto the pitch next week,” Taylor said, before admitting that “that’s going to be tight between now and the next international break.”

Anna Josendal, Sophie Román Haug and Aurélie Csillag face longer spells on the sidelines. “No other real issues, we should be pretty similar to what we were at West Ham,” the Liverpool boss said, pointing to Mari Ward and Lucy Parry building up their minutes. Viv Endemann and Mao Itamura are both working their way back after time out, with Taylor saying another week of training gets Itamura “a little bit closer to being really selectable.”

Liverpool were beaten 4-1 at West Ham last weekend and have not won in the league since a 2-0 victory over Everton at Anfield on 27 September. This is their second WSL fixture at the stadium this season.

These are the best laptops to buy right now, whether you're a professional or student

📅 2026-11-08  |  Read Source →

Sign up to Creative Bloq's daily newsletter, which brings you the latest news and inspiration from the worlds of art, design and technology.

Get full access to premium articles, exclusive features and a growing list of member rewards.

From design work to watching movies, these are the best laptops for every kind of pasttime.

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

If you need to buy a laptop for creative work, it's so important to get it right. Prices have climbed sharply this year, so you don't want to be saddled with the wrong thing..

Our top pick for graphic design is the MacBook Pro 14-inch (M6). It’s a brilliant all-rounder for almost any creative job. However, depending on what you need it for, other laptops are worth investigating too.

So in this article, I've highlighted the best laptops for a range of different tasks, based on hands-on testing by our reviewers using real design software. For further options, see our guides to the best laptops for graphic design and the best laptops for CAD.

The MacBook Pro has been the default laptop for designers for years, and nothing has yet changed that. The M5 model came first by a clear margin in our Photoshop benchmarks. Its screen is sharp and colour-accurate, and the battery comfortably lasts a working day. Now Apple has replaced it with the M6 version, which went on sale on 6 November.

We haven’t tested it yet, but it’s a spec bump rather than a redesign. Apple claims up to 30% faster graphics, and it adds Wi-Fi 7 and Bluetooth 6. Crucially, it costs the same as the M5 model, which rose to $1,999 / £1,999 in Apple’s June price rises.

So my advice is: either buy the M6 at full price, or pick up the M5 if you find it heavily discounted over Black Friday. Alternatively if money’s no object, Apple’s new MacBook Pro with a PROLED XDR display starts at $2,899 / £2,899 and adds a touchscreen. Our writer Daniel John called its OLED panel “the best laptop screen I’ve ever seen”.

The ProArt P16 is the best Windows laptop we’ve tested for creative work, and it's our top pick in both our laptop for CAD and laptop for animation guides.

If you're using CAD, the fact that this laptop runs Windows really matters. Revit and SolidWorks don’t run natively on macOS, while the P16’s Nvidia graphics and 64GB of memory give you plenty of headroom for large models. The built-in dial is handy for zooming and rotating, too.

If you're an animator, meanwhile, the star is the screen. It’s a colour-accurate OLED touchscreen, and as our reviewer Erlingur Einarsson says: “The 120Hz refresh rate is a welcome upgrade too, especially for animators and video editors.” The RTX 5070 also speeds up rendering in GPU-heavy tools.

The ASUS ProArt P16, in short, is pretty much everything a creative professional could hope for However, if you need even more power, ASUS also sells a version with an RTX 5090 and a 4K screen for $3,999.99.

The 16-inch MacBook Pro is the top pick in our 3D modelling laptop guide. You get a big, sharp screen, plenty of memory and the kind of battery life that 3D-capable Windows laptops can only dream of.

When our reviewer Rob Redman tested the previous M4 Pro model in Blender, Cinema 4D and Maya, he was impressed by its rendering speed and smooth viewport. “Simple things like dragging and dropping materials, which can be a little laggy on some systems, are perfectly smooth,” he says. The M5 Pro builds on that with a faster chip and Apple’s latest GPU cores.

Two things to bear in mind, though. First, if your pipeline relies on Nvidia’s CUDA or Windows-only plugins, you’re better off with a Windows machine. In which case, the Acer Nitro 16S AI is our best-value pick, while the RTX 5090 inside the Alienware 16 Area-51 is ideal if you need maximum rendering power.

Second, the MacBook Pro 16-inch (M5 Pro) is a lot of money, and the price climbs quickly once you add memory. If you have a lot of money, though, there's a very nice PROLED XDR version with a touchscreen costing $3,399.

Looking for the best laptop for Cricut? Then here's some good news. Cricut Design Space isn’t a demanding app, so you don’t need to spend a fortune on a laptop to use it.

Cricut recommends a minimum of an Intel Core i3 and 4GB of RAM, but 8GB is a more comfortable starting point, and 16GB will help with complex, multi-layer projects. The IdeaPad 5 2-in-1 gives you 16GB as standard, which is why it tops our Cricut laptop guide.

The 360-degree hinge is the clincher. You can fold the screen back into tablet mode and tap, drag and resize your designs directly on the screen. It also has a good range of ports, including USB-A, which is handy if you’re plugging in your cutting machine or a USB stick full of SVG files.

It’s a bit heavier than a regular laptop, and the screen isn’t the brightest or most colourful, but neither matters much for crafting.

The PZ13 is a Windows tablet with a detachable keyboard, and it’s the top pick in our drawing laptop guide. The big selling point is that ASUS includes the stylus, keyboard, stand and sleeve in the box, so the price you see is the price you pay.

The 13.3-inch OLED screen is bright, sharp and very colour-accurate, with a Delta-E of 0.3 in our testing. Battery life is excellent too, lasting over 20 hours in our video streaming test. “It’s unique and ideal for creatives,” says our reviewer.

It’s not built for processing power, though. Its Snapdragon chip is better suited to drawing and photo editing than 3D, and some Windows software, including InDesign, isn’t fully compatible with Snapdragon chips. So check your favourite apps run well on it before you buy. The glossy screen can also be reflective.

For most students, and certainly for creative ones, the MacBook Air is the laptop to get. It’s thin, light and silent, the battery easily lasts all day, and it has enough power for design, photo editing and light video work. “A thin and light laptop that performs as well as this will always be attractive to a creative audience,” says our reviewer Ian Evenden.

That power is the reason to choose it over the cheaper MacBook Neo. In our testing, the Air M5 was much closer to the MacBook Pro than the Neo: it scored 17,091 for multi-core performance in Geekbench 6, against 17,897 for the M5 MacBook Pro. In Photoshop, it more than doubled the Neo’s scores.

It’s not so cheap any more, though. Apple raised the price by $200 / £200 in June, although students can get quite a lot of money off through Apple’s education store. If you want to save cash and your course doesn’t involve creative software, the MacBook Neo at $699 / £699 is a great-value alternative, as long as you can live with 8GB of memory.

The MacBook Pro 14-inch (M6). It’s fast, has a superb screen, lasts all day on a charge, and it handles everything from design and illustration to photo and video editing. If you prefer Windows, the ASUS ProArt P16 is the best alternative.

It depends mainly on your software. Most design, photo and video apps run on both, and Macs win on battery life and build quality. But if you work in CAD, Windows is the safer bet, as Revit and SolidWorks don’t run natively on macOS. Windows laptops are also the only option for Nvidia graphics, which many 3D and rendering tools rely on.

For creative work, 16GB is the minimum we’d recommend, and 32GB is the sweet spot if you work with large files. You’ll only need 64GB or more for heavy 3D, video or AI work. Note that RAM on Macs and many thin Windows laptops can’t be upgraded later, so buy what you’ll need.

Not for graphic design, illustration or Cricut crafting, where integrated graphics are fine. For 3D, CAD, animation and video editing, a powerful GPU will save you lots of time, which means either an Nvidia RTX graphics card or a MacBook Pro with an M5 Pro or Max chip.

For drawing and Cricut work, yes. Being able to tap, drag and sketch directly on the screen makes both much more natural. For most other creative work, it’s a nice extra rather than an essential.

Cricut says that Design Space isn’t supported on Chromebooks, so we’d recommend a Windows laptop or a Mac. Some users run the Android version of the app on a Chromebook, but you may lose some features.

Spalletti skips Fiorentina preview as Juventus seek a first win over Vanoli

📅 2026-11-08  |  Read Source →

Luciano Spalletti will not hold his eve-of-match press conference before Sunday's trip to Fiorentina. Juventus arrive on two straight wins, chasing fourth place, against a coach Spalletti has never beaten.

Juventus will travel to Florence without the usual preview from their coach. Sunday’s game at the Franchi kicks off at 20:45. The squad got back to Turin overnight from the Netherlands. The reason given is the club’s crowded run of fixtures, TuttoJuve reported. It is the last game before the international break.

The run began with a 2-0 win over Napoli at the Allianz Stadium on 1 November. Kerim Alajbegovic scored in the 70th minute. Aged 19 years and 41 days, no foreign player had ever found the net at a younger age in a Serie A game between Juventus and Napoli.

Four days later, Juventus won 2-1 away at AZ Alkmaar in the Europa League. Teun Koopmeiners and Randal Kolo Muani scored. The result takes them to 10 points from four games in the competition. After the match, Spalletti described himself as very pleased with a solid team display against strong opponents. He felt his side should have added a third goal.

The league position still needs work. The club’s stated aim this season is fourth place, and Como currently hold that spot. The defence gives some grounds for confidence. Going back to last March, no side can match the Bianconeri’s tally of 12 shutouts. Roma are next on 11.

Vanoli and Spalletti have faced each other only twice as coaches. Both meetings were Fiorentina-Juventus fixtures last season. Last November they drew 1-1 at the Franchi. Filip Kostic gave Juventus the lead before Rolando Mandragora equalised with a long-range shot.

The hosts’ struggles on their own pitch offer Juventus an opening against a side yet to find its footing at home.

The fixture carries plenty of history. Sunday’s game is the 175th Serie A meeting between the sides, according to the club’s statistical preview. Juventus have won 82, Fiorentina 37, and 55 have ended level. Juventus have scored 275 top-flight goals against Fiorentina. Two more would draw them level with Inter’s 277, the highest total by any side against the Viola.

Once the international break is over, Juventus’s next Serie A match is on 22 November.

How do porta potties work?

📅 2026-11-08  |  Read Source →

Published Nov 8, 2026 8:03 AM EST

We’ve all used them. Whether at festivals, campgrounds, construction sites, or even weddings, porta potties are an unavoidable—and often underappreciated—part of modern life. They’re the unsung heroes of moments when bathrooms are few and far between, faithfully answering nature’s call, one flushless visit at a time.

But how do porta potties work, exactly? They’re so commonplace—and so universally dreaded—that most people try to get in, do their business, and get out as quickly as possible, without giving much thought to what’s happening. But plug your nose, lift the lid, and brave a peek inside, and you’ll find a simple yet elegant system designed to reliably meet one of humanity’s most basic needs.

Conventional toilets rely on a combination of water, pressure, and gravity to do their job. When you flush, water rushes from the tank into the bowl, pushing waste down through a curved pipe called a trap and into the plumbing system.

From there, the wastewater travels through sewer pipes to a treatment plant or, in some instances, a septic system. A small amount of water remains behind in the trap, creating a seal that prevents sewer gases from wafting back into the bathroom.

Once the process is complete, the tank refills with clean water from the plumbing system, typically from a municipal water supply or private well, and the porcelain throne is ready for its next user.

Portable toilets—also known as portaloos, portajohns, and a variety of other nicknames—use water, too. But that’s about where the similarities end. “There’s no plumbing, and it really doesn’t smell good,” says Walt Terry, director of operations for Metro Services, a family-owned portable toilet and dumpster rental company in Little Rock, Arkansas. “It’s not the most pleasant thing in the world.”

Modern porta potties come in a variety of shapes and sizes, from swanky multi-stall trailers to no-frills cubicles. The standard plastic model you see at events has a relatively simple design, one that traces its roots to George Hardig. Hardig filed a late-1960s patent for porta potties. Later he became a co-founder of PolyJohn Enterprises, still one of the leading portable toilet manufacturers today.

Made of large, rigid plastic panels, Hardig’s invention was designed to be weather- and insect-proof, simple to clean and maintain, and lightweight and easy to move. His model was cylindrical and featured a removable toilet tank, while today’s are usually rectangular and have the holding tank built-in. But other than that, not much has changed in porta potty design since Hardig first developed his portable toilet “cabana” nearly six decades ago.

These tall boxes have a large empty cavity at the back, topped by a toilet seat. When users do their business, waste simply drops down into the large, roomy hole beneath.

“It’s just a big void space,” says Katie Gossett, office manager for Tidy Tim’s, Inc., a portable restroom and septic service company her dad started more than 30 years ago in central Ohio.

“The holding tanks can vary a bit in size, but your average is like 25 to 30 gallons.”

The holding tank is filled with some amount of water, typically between five and 10 gallons, as well as a special, dark-blue chemical cocktail that serves numerous purposes. It helps keep the funky odors under control and starts to break down the waste and toilet paper, while also hiding the tank’s contents. The color is also a key indicator of a porta potty’s freshness.

“It will actually turn green once it’s not working anymore—when it gets there, it’s not a good sight,” says Terry. “The chemical goes in there and does as much as it can, until it can’t.”

There’s another easily overlooked component that helps keep porta potties from getting too stinky: the vent pipe. The next time you use a portable toilet, look up at the roof. You’ll spot a small pipe protruding from the back corner, discreetly drawing foul gases out of the holding tank and venting them into the air.

Some models also have built-in urinals, which simply funnel urine into the holding tank for those who prefer to remain standing when they pee.

When it’s time to service a porta potty, specially trained employees from the rental company arrive with a powerful vacuum truck. They use a hose to suck out the tank’s liquids and solids, transferring the waste into the truck for disposal.

Many vacuum trucks also have a separate compartment filled with clean water, which staffers then use to wash out the porta potty’s entire interior—the tank, as well as the walls, ceiling, and floors.

Sanitizing spray and a quick wipedown with a microfiber towel finishes the job—which is a lot less gross than you might imagine. “Our guys know what they’re doing,” says Gossett. “I’m not going to say accidents don’t happen, but most of the time, when they get [back to the office], they are exactly the way they left. They very rarely get dirty.”

What happens when driverless cars break the law?

The surprising science behind Japan’s vending machine obsession

No, cruise ships aren’t floating petri dishes

Airplane seats really are getting smaller

Rental companies typically service their units on a regular schedule, going around to the various porta potties they own and sucking out all the waste. Once the vacuum truck is full, they drive to a local wastewater treatment facility and dump it.

“It’s like a city sewer plant that is able to add some to their load,” says Gossett. “Their treatment plant is big enough that they can take on that extra waste and process it for us.”

The servicing schedule varies, but a good rule of thumb is that one portable restroom can accommodate 10 people during a typical 40-hour workweek before it needs to be cleaned out.

And, no, unless you go totally crazy, your porta potty probably isn’t going to overflow.

“A lot of people worry that they’re going to fill up, but usually that’s not the case,” says Kade Jensen, the owner of Minty Pine Sanitation Solutions Ltd. in southern Alberta, Canada.

Porta potties have gotten a bad rap over the years. Yet there’s an entire corner of the internet that’s apparently fascinated by what happens when they get cleaned out.

A post shared by Minty Pine Cleans (@mintypinecleans)

Jensen, who is 23, found that out a few months ago, when he started posting regular point-of-view videos of his porta potty cleanouts on social media. Each one starts with a direct look into the full tank, but ends with the oddly satisfying payoff of a squeaky-clean unit.

Since then, he’s watched his company’s follower count skyrocket, and his views climb into the hundreds of millions. “A lot of people have always seen [porta potties] and used them, but they don’t know the exact process,” he says.

“It’s almost like people want to look away but they can’t look away at the same time. It’s something you’re not used to seeing.”

When he first started posting videos, Jensen says, he was simply having fun. But the response has given him a new perspective: He now sees the videos as a way to pull back the curtain on an industry that most people would rather not think about—and show that portable restrooms aren’t nearly as revolting as their reputation suggests.

“Our whole thing is that it really doesn’t have to be gross,” he says. “It’s really not as scary or gross as people make it out to be.”

Bitcoin price prediction — With $80K lost, is more downside ahead for BTC?

📅 2026-11-08  |  Read Source →

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Despite rebounding slightly above the $80,000-level, Bitcoin failed to hold above this level. In fact, it has since fallen to the $75K-level, where it has been consolidating for the last three days.

This relief rally was partly driven by liquidation of sell orders, which in turn produced a short squeeze. According to a CryptoQuant report, more than $290 million in shorts were wiped out, amplifying the uptick.

Furthermore, the stablecoin supply fell by about 5% over the past month, implying part of the capital was being swapped for BTC among other cryptos.

For instance, the Drift Protocol hacker went long after swapping $13 million USD Coin [USDC] on Hyperliquid for Bitcoin. Some large wallets were also accumulating the token even though the apparent demand remained negative, showing the market did not absorb available BTC.

However, Bitcoin did not hold above $80K despite this mild buying activity. This observation could be attributed to a couple of on-chain factors.

The report further observed that spot demand has remained weak, as the monthly average net spot volume delta was at -$28 million and -$9.40 million on Binance and Coinbase, respectively.

In addition, short-term holders have been moving their holdings to exchanges this month. While these transfers of about 32.70K BTC do not prove sales, they do imply looming selling pressure.

Moreover, institutional demand through BTC ETFs fell flat, with $412 million in net outflows recorded. The largest single outflow came from BlackRock’s IBIT, where traders sold $403 million in BTC.

Further analysis of the Hyperliquid exchange revealed that whales have been adding shorts. The net positioning turned bearish after seven months of being bullish.

The last time BTC had such a huge short exposure was in October 2025, when the price crashed to $60K thereafter. These bearish bets have simply added to the pressure.

Finally, the Coinbase Premium Index, which tracks both Coinbase and Binance for the U.S market, has stayed negative.

BTC’s price action at press time revealed a sideways consolidation since the crash to $75,000. The returning mild accumulation could offset bearish positioning and sustain a recovery.

However, there need to be improvements in the apparent demand, ETF inflows, and U.S buying pressure.

Muriuki Lazaro is a on-chain data analyst with a B.Sc. in Data Science. Muriuki specializes in dissecting complex on-chain data into clear and accurate insights for readers in the crypto ecosystem, with a particular focus on Bitcoin.

Digital Foundry Delivers Its Tech Verdict On Stellar Blade's Switch 2 Port

📅 2026-11-08  |  Read Source →

Stellar Blade also arrived on the Switch 2 this week, and it's been positively received so far. Here on Nintendo Life, we gave it a score of seven out of ten, and although there are some technical drawbacks, Eve's outing stands tall on the hybrid platform.

The team at Digital Foundry has now delivered its tech review of the Unreal Engine 4 title, and while it's no "tour de force", it does translate to a "better than expected Switch 2 port", with what's described as an experience with "gentler compromises" than many more recent titles.

"Technically, it's not a tour de force either, with more conservative visual technology that doesn't leverage current gen hardware like many other high budget titles, but that does translate to a better than expected Switch 2 port, with gentler compromises than many more recent titles. Not everything survives intact, but the game's visual character isn't disrupted by the excision of key lighting systems or anything like that. Instead, players can expect a range of more iterative asset downgrades, some of which only really stand out in head-to-heads."

As for frame generation, on Switch 2, Digital Foundry says it's "not a bad choice considering the CPU and GPU limits of the console", but in the case of Stellar Blade, a "40fps targeting option" may have offered a better experience than the 60fps output with frame gen on. You can also turn frame gen "off" and the game will hover around the 40fps mark.

Overall, though, Stellar Blade is summed up as a "reasonable" and "playable experience" on Nintendo's new system. You can get the full rundown in the video above. There's also a demo on the eShop, and you find out more about how it performs in our review here on Nintendo Life:

Review Stellar Blade: Complete Edition (Switch 2) - Perfectly Playable, Despite Drawbacks

How has your experience been with Stellar Blade on the Switch 2 so far? Let us know in the comments.

Liam is a news writer and reviewer on Nintendo Life. He's been writing about games for more than 15 years and is a lifelong fan of many iconic video game characters.

I bought the physical copy. I’d played the demo to the end. Yesterday I defeated the boss that soon followed. It took a few attempts but got it in the end. It runs more than well enough, like similar fast-paced action games, Onimusha Way of the Sword and Dynasty Warriors Origins.

Good to have further confirmation about this - my copy arrived yesterday so I'm definitely playing Stellar Blade at some point (not immediately since I'm currently playing too many games as is but still, I'm quite looking forward to it)!

I highly recommend everyone disable the frame gen. This is a rare instance where running the game at 60FPS is actually worse because it causes some nasty input lag.

Hopefully this is something they can fix in a future update, but for now you're better off without the frame gen.

I watched the video and it’s generally pretty positive. Importantly, they say that VRR is working properly in handheld (one of the few games where this is the case according to them). So frame gen off in handheld isn’t overly stuttery even with the frame rate fluctuating around 40fps. No VRR implementation in docked though, but as it’s a relatively new feature they may be planning to patch it in and that would be fantastic.

For handheld play, the best option seems to be No Frame Gen. With framerates most often above 40 fps, VRR works and makes it a smooth experience. And the input latency should be minimal.

I don’t get this wish for a 40 fps limit when VRR is available and the framerate rarely exceeds 50 fps.

Arsenal 1-0 Hull: Not efficient, but enough

📅 2026-11-08  |  Read Source →

In yesterday’s preview blog, I spoke about Arsenal’s attacking efficiency, and how that might be key to taking all three points. In yesterday’s game against Hull, Arsenal took 21 shots, 10 of which were on target, and scored one goal from that. Efficient?

Despite generally dominating possession, I thought there was a lack of precision in certain aspects of our first half play. Passes weren’t quite quick enough or accurate enough, but there were moments when we could have found the breakthrough. Gabriel almost got on the end of a Noni Madueke cross which was flicked on towards the back post, Rice had a tame shot from a free kick before a late header which he probably should have done better with.

And then there was Kai Havertz. It’s fair to say Hull didn’t leave a lot of room behind a defensive line that often had six players stretched across it, but late in the half a superb Martin Odegaard pass sent him clean through, one on one with the keeper. I haven’t seen replays but in real time I felt like he should have pulled the trigger – instead he over-complicated it. You have to be prepared to shoot from there, even if it’s on your wrong foot.

Then there was a penalty incident when he complained vociferously, play went on, the ref was then told to go to the screen and we all assumed it would be a penalty. However, the ref said it was a foul but Havertz was offside – and it felt like a massive waste of time. Afterwards, it emerged it should have been a penalty, but apparently the rule is ‘open to interpretation’. So clear as mud then.

In the second half, we seemed to play down our right a lot, with Madueke the outlet most of the time. I thought he did pretty well, much better than Christos Tzolis on the other side, but the breakthrough wouldn’t come. Mikel Merino replaced Rice (who seemed to be on the end of a wild challenge in midfield that the ref just ignored as he tumbled through the air), and Viktor Gyokeres came on for Havertz.

On 71 minutes though, we found the goal. Madueke’s shot was spilled by the keeper, Odegaard was quickest to react to poke the ball home for 1-0. And to be fair, you always felt confident that would be enough. We are the masters of the single goal margin win, and this was another one. Not a day that will live long in the memory or anything, but a 1-0 win that takes us into the Interlull in really good shape, and there’s not much arguing with that.

Or really, much more to say about this game. Mikel Arteta sounded happy enough, saying:

I’m delighted with the win. To go into the international break with a win is one of the best things about being a coach or player, I would say. Regarding the performance, I thought we were the better team, had enough chances to score and make it bigger for ourselves and we weren’t efficient enough to take them.

We then had to rely on generating volume, plenty of persisting until we finally broke them down. Martin then got the goal. Not the most beautiful one, but the most effective one, a simple ‘shoot and then follow the next action’, and that was ultimately enough to win the game.

So Arsenal sit top of the table, with 9 wins from 10 so far this season in the league, and it’s a very impressive run of form. Do I wish we had more attacking potency? Absolutely, and I do worry a bit that it might bite us in the arse somewhere down the line, but for now, we can see that this is a team that just knows how to win, and perhaps that’s the most important thing.

Right, I’m gonna leave it there for this morning. I have to head back to Dublin today. It was great to see lots of you over the weekend, until next time! And we’ll have an Arsecast Extra for you tomorrow too.

Air France A350 Nearly Collides Midair With American A321 After Wrong Turn At LAX

📅 2026-11-08  |  Read Source →

Journalist – Aaron is an aviation and travel journalist with 14 years of experience in the travel and tourism industry. His background spans airport operations, aviation infrastructure, and travel consulting, having worked with global organizations across New Zealand, Europe, and North America. Combining industry expertise with firsthand travel experience across more than 52 countries and five continents, Aaron covers airlines, airports, aviation policy, and the wider travel sector. He is based in Canterbury, New Zealand. His work has been published in Simple Flying and featured by MSN, SAM CHUI Aviation & Travel, EIN News, Pakistan Defence, and Climate Depot.

This article was updated on Monday, November 9, 2026, to include new details on the unauthorized turn and a similar 2025 incident. It was originally published on Sunday, November 8, 2026.

Two aircraft belonging to American Airlines and Air France came within 400 feet (121 meters) of vertical separation as both jets departed from Los Angeles International Airport (LAX). Quick actions by air traffic control and the pilots onboard averted a close call that could have had catastrophic consequences.

The two planes were departing from California's busiest airport in the early hours of Saturday morning. Air France Flight 28 was heading for Papeete (PPT), while American Flight 1660 was flying to Dallas/Fort Worth International Airport (DFW). The two jets — which collectively could have been carrying more than 500 passengers — came dangerously close to each other shortly after takeoff, before controllers intervened to prevent a potential collision.

The Air France Airbus A350-900, operating as AF28, had been cleared to take off from Los Angeles via the SUMMR2 procedure. SUMMR2, according to the Federal Aviation Administration (FAA), is a set route that tells departing aircraft which direction to fly after takeoff. In this case, planes taking off from Runways 24L/R and 25L/R must initially climb straight ahead before following their assigned route.

At the same time, the American jet, destined for Texas, was told by air traffic control to stay at 2,000 feet, to allow the Air France widebody to turn and then head to FL270. However, an unauthorized turn by the Air France aircraft brought the two jets onto conflicting flight paths.

Simple Flying reached out to Air France for comment on the incident; however, an airline spokesperson could not immediately be reached.

According to TurbineTraveler on X, the Air France plane made an unauthorized turn left shortly after takeoff, rather than continuing straight as instructed. This placed the A350 directly in the trajectory of the departing American jet. This led the two planes to come within dangerous proximity of each other, known as a loss of separation event.

The controller quickly spotted the developing conflict and instructed both aircraft to turn away from each other. The controller then questioned the Air France crew about the deviation from its departure route. Both aircraft involved are detailed in the table below, with flight data captured from the Simple Flying Flight Tracker:

Both the American and Air France flights were then able to continue safely to their intended destinations with no reported injuries or harm to anyone on board. A similar incident occurred in Los Angeles in 2025, when an American and ITA Airways jet almost collided midair after a pilot deviation from the busy airport.

Two aircraft lost separation while climbing out of LAX.

So far, reports indicate that neither crew received a Traffic Collision Avoidance System (TCAS) resolution advisory, an automated cockpit warning that instructs pilots to climb or descend to avoid another aircraft. These alerts can prevent midair collisions when aircraft get too close, giving pilots immediate instructions to increase their separation.

Since the incident was first reported, outlets have published different figures for how close AF28 and AA1660 came to each other. According to The Aviation Herald, the A350 began turning left while climbing through 1,800 feet, despite its departure procedure calling for a straight climb to 5,000 feet.

The incident also bears striking similarities to an event involving ITA Airways in October 2025, when a Rome-bound Airbus A330-900 departing Runway 24L turned left toward an American A321 that had taken off from Runway 25R.

As one of the busiest airports in the US and the world, Los Angeles International Airport has carefully controlled departure routes to keep aircraft safely separated. The SUMMR2 procedure directs westbound departures out over the Pacific Ocean, helping keep them clear of other departing and arriving traffic.

SUMMR flights often transition to long-haul or coastal routes, including to destinations in Hawaii, Asia, and the north, like San Francisco. Instead of following this procedure, the Air France pilot made a sharp left turn shortly after departure, which intersected the path of the American jet that had departed from Runway 25R. Fortunately, the departure controller identified the conflict, and their swift actions resulted in the two planes being able to quickly change course and avoid a midair collision.

Mammograms May Offer Early Insight Into Cardiovascular Disease

📅 2026-11-08  |  Read Source →

Follow Healthline on our socials for all things health.

Mammograms are widely used to help detect breast cancer, but they may also offer insight into heart health.

The routine X-ray screening may identify breast arterial calcification (BAC), which is the presence of calcium deposits within the walls of the small arteries in the breast.

On a mammogram, BAC is commonly identified as thin, white, parallel lines that give a “tram-track” appearance.

While BAC is harmless to the breasts, it is associated with a significantly higher risk of cardiovascular disease. A 2022 study found women with BAC were 51% more likely to develop heart disease or experience a stroke than those without it.

Laxmi Mehta, MD, a cardiologist at The Ohio State University Wexner Medical Center, said BAC is associated with cardiovascular risk factors, such as diabetes, high blood pressure, and high cholesterol. Additionally, BAC has been connected to:

“The reasons for BAC resulting in higher risks for cardiovascular disease are not definitely determined; however, there are some potential mechanisms, one being that they share similar risk factors that result in vascular injury or metabolic stress that result in vascular calcification and atherosclerosis,” Mehta told Healthline.

Cheng-Han Chen, MD, interventional cardiologist and medical director of the Structural Heart Program at MemorialCare Saddleback Medical Center, added that because BAC and heart disease share many of the same risk factors, “calcium in breast arteries therefore suggests disease in the heart (coronary) and brain arteries as well,” he told Healthline.

While 1 in 39 females in the United States dies from breast cancer each year, cardiovascular disease causes one out of every three deaths, resulting in 1 death per minute.

“Cardiovascular disease is still the most common cause of death in premenopausal women,” Richard Reitherman, MD, PhD, a radiologist and medical director of breast imaging at MemorialCare Breast Center at Orange Coast Medical Center, told Healthline.

“The presentation of cardiovascular disease in premenopausal women is notoriously different than in postmenopausal women, primarily [because] there are no typical symptoms such as chest pain.”

Reitherman said that a prevalent and common health screening procedure such as a mammogram can lead to the early identification of women with silent cardiovascular disease.

“Cardiovascular outcome studies that have included breast arterial calcifications as a risk factor are increasingly reported in the medical literature,” he said.

“The hope is that identifying younger women with silent cardiovascular risks [like BAC along with] appropriate integrated risk identification, analysis, and interventions can significantly reduce mortality in this age group.”

Mehta said there are several reasons BAC has been overlooked as a risk factor for heart disease.

“Mammograms are completed for assessment of breast cancer, and BAC has been historically an ‘incidental’ finding, which is not clinically relevant from an oncologic standpoint,” she said.

Additionally, there is no standardization on quantification of BAC severity, nor is it a required component of mammogram reports, said Mehta.

However, routine reporting of BAC on mammograms is an urgent goal of the American College of Radiology, Society of Breast Imaging, and Medicare, said Reitherman.

In fact, it is gaining traction in the United States. A new Maryland law, passed on October 1, requires mammography centers to notify women when a BAC is detected on their mammogram.

“Automated identification and reporting of these calcifications detected on screening mammograms will become increasingly prevalent in the coming months [and] years,” said Reitherman.

The next step is to identify this group of women to primary care physicians so that appropriate risk evaluation can take place, Reitherman added.

In addition to doctors routinely screening for conditions related to heart disease, such as diabetes, high blood pressure, and high cholesterol, he hopes BAC will also be routinely considered.

“Under current development are protocols to identify patients needing a cardiologist’s training and expertise to appropriately stratify this group of patients,” said Reitherman.

“The common healthcare behavior [of] having regular mammograms not only reduces risk of morbidity and mortality from breast cancer but will hopefully contribute to the reduction of morbidity and mortality from cardiovascular disease in younger women.”

He said this type of integrated healthcare approach demonstrates cross-specialty integration and coordination among women, radiologists, and their healthcare providers to improve women’s lives.

“This is an amazing game-changing opportunity,” Reitherman said.

Mehta said it’s reasonable for women to ask if BAC is present on their mammogram report.

“But if it is not uniformly reported by the radiologist, the individual clinician may not have the answer,” she said.

More research is needed before BAC on mammograms can be used routinely to predict heart disease.

Finding out your BAC could provide additional information to help you and your doctors decide how aggressively to address your cardiovascular risk factors, Chen said.

Reitherman agreed. “The goal of reducing morbidity and mortality from any cause such as cardiovascular disease is based on recognition of risk factors, followed by appropriate lifestyle and pharmacologic interventions,” he said.

Güler scores and assists as Real Madrid win at Mestalla to draw level with Barcelona

📅 2026-11-08  |  Read Source →

Arda Güler scored once and set up another as José Mourinho's Real Madrid beat Valencia 3-0 to reach 30 points, level with Barcelona, before the international break. Valencia are second from bottom in their last season at Mestalla.

Federico Valverde put Madrid ahead at Mestalla in the 28th minute on Saturday 7 November 2026. Güler doubled the lead eight minutes later. Jude Bellingham reached the byline and pulled the ball back. Güler met it first time and finished with his right foot.

Güler could have had more. Stole Dimitrievski stopped him twice: a strong shot from the edge of the area in the 43rd minute and a one-on-one in the 54th.

Kylian Mbappé’s strike in the 75th minute made it three. That goal stretched his scoring run to seven matches in a row. He has 12 league goals, three behind Barcelona’s Raphinha at the top of the scoring chart.

The visitors had 68% of the ball and completed 568 passes to Valencia’s 225. They took 19 shots, 11 of them on target. Valencia managed four shots and just two on target. Expected goals finished 2.39 to 0.26 in Madrid’s favour.

The hosts’ best moment came around the 20th minute. Aaron Mayol won the ball just outside the area and forced Thibaut Courtois to stretch for a save. Aguirre sent on Luis Rioja for Justin de Haas at half-time, but the pattern did not change.

The game grew bad-tempered. Valencia committed 26 fouls to Madrid’s six, and Aguirre himself was booked for dissent in the 59th minute. In the club’s own view, the punishment for Gayà’s challenge on Mbappé was harsh. Seven yellow cards were shown in all, four of them in a two-minute spell after the red card.

Two wins from 12 league games is all Valencia have to show so far. Only Malaga, on 5 points, are below them. Aguirre has one win from his five league games in charge. The club is already promoting season-ticket prices for the new Nou Mestalla.

The coach is concerned about the home form. Aguirre told EFE he was worried about failing to win any of his three matches at Mestalla. He added that Valencia were short of points with six games left in the first half of the season.

Matteo Thun designs Torre Velasca interiors using "building itself as the starting point"

📅 2026-11-08  |  Read Source →

Photography by Alberto Strada (unless stated otherwise)

Italian architect Matteo Thun looked to the original materials and colours of Milan’s iconic Torre Velasca when creating its new interiors, which feature pink hues inspired by the tower’s dusky facade.

Thun has designed 72 flats in the upper floors of the tower, ranging from suites to duplex penthouses. He approached the interior design of the skyscraper, built in the 1950s by the architecture studio BBPR, with a focus on reinterpretation.

“We allowed the masters and the building itself to speak, imagining a way of living within it that would carry the building a little further into the future, adding a new chapter to its history,” Thun told Dezeen.

“We did not seek contrast: our design was informed directly by the materials and colours of Torre Velasca, and we chose not to intervene where we felt the architecture had already achieved a sense of completeness.”

His studio preserved original details, including doors, window frames, loggias and tiles in the bathrooms and kitchens. These were then juxtaposed with modern furniture and evergreen design classics.

“The interiors bring together new furniture designed by the studio and design references spanning the 1950s to the 1980s, including iconic pieces developed in new colourways in collaboration with historic Milanese companies,” Thun said.

“With a deep appreciation for craftsmanship and a strong attention to detail, these designs share an ability to evolve without losing their identity.”

The colour palette, too, was inspired by the original hues of the Torre Velasca.

In the living rooms, walls are covered in custom-made velvet wall coverings by textile company Dedar in a pale pink colour that resembles the pink plaster on Torre Velasca’s facade.

“The building itself was our starting point,” Thun said.

“The pink plaster, developed and patented specifically for the tower, together with the blue and green floor tiles found both inside and in the loggias, formed the basis of our colour palette,” he continued.

“We developed different gradations of these tones, opting for darker shades of green and blue to define the entrance areas and bedrooms. “

As well as the apartments, Thun also designed the interior of the reception, lobby and the corridors on the ground floor.

And while this was an interior project, Thun says it ended up becoming “a lesson in architecture” for his studio.

“Working with a building as distinctive as Torre Velasca challenged us to think and work first and foremost as architects,” Thun said.

“The challenge was not to reinvent the building, but to find ways to make its original character feel relevant today, while remaining true to BBPR’s vision,” he continued.

“In this sense, the project became a process of discovery, with the building itself serving as our greatest teacher. It was, in many ways, a lesson in architecture for us.”

Thun has previously designed the interior of the boutique hotel The Julius Prague and “hotel-like” rooms for a German hospital.

Photography by Alberto Strada (unless stated otherwise)

Video: Zelda: Ocarina Of Time Side-By-Side Graphics Comparison (Switch 2, 3DS, N64)

📅 2026-11-08  |  Read Source →

Zelda: Ocarina of Time returned this week on the Switch 2 and it looks better than ever. If you are wondering just much of a facelift it's had since the 2011 outing, and the original N64 hit, you might want to check out this side-by-side comparison video.

YouTube channel ElAnalistDeBits has now released a "final graphics comparison" which takes a closer look at the character models, environments, physics, the many locations in the game, and much more.

If you are wondering about the performance, the remake on Switch 2 runs at 60fps. In comparison, the original N64 outing ran at 20fps. And as for the resolution, this new remake ranges from 1080p to 1440p in docked, and 720p to 1080p in handheld (via Digital Foundry).

In our review of the Ocarina of Time remake, we noted it was the most visually stunning work Nintendo has ever released:

"Ocarina of Time on Switch 2 is a stellar reimagining of the original, one that completely revamps the experience with stunning visuals, modernised gameplay, and vastly improved audio. Despite some minor blemishes, this is comfortably the most visually stunning work Nintendo has ever put out, and it’s unquestionably the best way to play one of the most seminal games of all time."

Review The Legend of Zelda: Ocarina of Time Remake - One of Nintendo's Best-Looking Games Ever

How are you finding the remake so far? What do you think of it compared to the N64 and 3DS versions? Let us know in the comments.

“Despite some minor blemishes, this is comfortably the most visually stunning work Nintendo has ever put out”

More than that, it’s the most visually stunning game ever. Somehow they managed to undersell the richness of the environments, especially Kakariko Village.

3DS version's aesthetics are definitely my favorite of the three. The Switch 2 one's certainly the most technically impressive but still not really that fond of how glossy everyone is and think most of the areas are overdesigned. Game also can't really seem to make up its mind if it's trying to expand on the look of the original, trying to look like BotW, or trying to do its own thing and it feels inconsistent at times as a result.

Good example of that off the top of my head, Ruto's overall look - especially her face - being very clearly retooled to more closely resemble the BotW Zoras, while Nabooru has a giant square nose and round eyes that while they kinda align with BotW are a lot more stiff and puppet-like to the point she more closely resembles her N64 model.
